People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Honestly, the thing that kind of pushed me to come in is my eating has been really off lately. It’s not even in one direction, which is why it’s been bothering me. Some days I’ll get to the end of the day and realize I barely ate anything, like I just forgot meals were a thing, and then other nights it’s the total opposite — I’m up late and suddenly I’m going through takeout, snacks, whatever’s around, way after midnight.

It’s embarrassing because it sounds simple, like “just eat normally,” but it hasn’t felt normal. It’s been all over the place enough that I started thinking, okay, maybe I should actually talk to somebody instead of pretending it’s just a weird week.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not really, no. I mean, it’s not like I sit there planning it out, like “I’m going to eat all this food tonight.” It’s more like I’ll suddenly realize it’s after midnight and I’m just tearing through whatever’s there — takeout, snacks, stuff I bought earlier and forgot about — and then afterward I’m like, why did I do that?

And then the weird part is other days are the total opposite. I’ll go most of the day and it’s like meals don’t even register. I’ll look up and realize I basically forgot to eat. So the late-night stuff feels extra out of control because it’s not even consistent. It’s not just that I’m hungry at night — my eating in general has been all over the place, and that’s honestly what scared me enough to come in.

I don’t know if I’d call it guilt exactly. Maybe embarrassed, yeah, because it feels kind of ridiculous afterward, like I should’ve been able to just stop or make a normal choice. But it’s not like I’m sitting there hating myself for it every single time.

It’s more this uncomfortable feeling of, “What was that?” Like I’ll look at the wrappers or the empty containers and feel kind of grossed out and annoyed with myself, but also confused, because in the moment it doesn’t feel that dramatic. Then afterward it feels like I wasn’t really paying attention to what I was doing. So… ashamed might be too strong, but embarrassed and frustrated, definitely.

I haven’t really kept track on a scale, so I can’t say like, “I gained exactly this much” or “lost exactly this much.” But I wouldn’t be surprised if it’s changed some, because my eating has been so uneven.

That’s honestly the part that scared me into coming in — it’s not just a normal appetite shift. Some days I’ll realize I’ve basically skipped meals without meaning to, like I got through the whole day and food just didn’t occur to me. And then other nights it flips completely, and I’m up after midnight tearing through takeout and snacks like I can’t get a handle on it.

So weight-wise, I’m not sure of the number, but appetite-wise and eating pattern-wise, yeah, something has definitely been off.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean… I guess I worry a little, but it’s not exactly the weight part that’s been scaring me the most. Like, I haven’t been obsessing over “am I gaining weight” after every night or anything like that. It’s more that the whole pattern feels off and I don’t feel like I understand what my body is doing.

Because some days I’ll honestly just forget meals completely — like I’ll get through most of the day and then realize I didn’t really eat anything normal. And then other nights, especially after midnight, it’s like I’m suddenly tearing through takeout and snacks, just whatever’s around, and it feels way bigger than normal hunger.

So yeah, weight crosses my mind, like, “This probably can’t be good for me,” but the bigger worry is how all over the place it’s gotten. That’s the part that made me think I should actually talk to somebody instead of just assuming it’ll even out.

Not really in the sense of, like, “I can’t eat bread” or “I’m avoiding dessert because I’m scared I’ll gain weight.” It hasn’t been that organized, I guess. Honestly, the thing that scared me into coming in is more that my eating has been all over the place.

That’s the part that made me think I should actually talk to somebody instead of just assuming it’ll even out.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid eating certain foods because you're afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not really in the sense of, like, “I can’t eat bread” or “I’m avoiding dessert because I’m scared I’ll gain weight.” It hasn’t been that organized, I guess. Honestly, the thing that scared me into coming in is more that my eating has been all over the place.\n\nLike some days I’ll just forget meals completely, not because I’m trying to restrict or anything, but because I just don’t think about it or I’m distracted and then suddenly it’s late. And then other nights it goes the total opposite way, where after midnight I’m tearing through takeout and snacks, just eating whatever’s there. So it’s less about avoiding certain foods and more like I don’t feel like I have a normal pattern right now.\n\n[Last Question]\nHas your irregular eating pattern been interfering with your daily activities or relationships?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 26 / sales_representative_wholesale_or_manufacturing / high_school\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Functional Impairment\nmanifestation: \"My manager has started pulling me off accounts because I’m making mistakes I normally wouldn’t, and HR got involved after the employee-assistance call. I’m still showing up, but work and home both feel like they’re slipping out of my control.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… it has. I mean, I’m still functioning on the surface — I’m still getting to work, I’m still answering messages, still trying to keep things looking normal — but it’s starting to show in ways I can’t really explain away anymore.

At work, my manager has started pulling me off some accounts because I’m making mistakes I normally wouldn’t make. Like little details I’d usually catch, follow-ups I’d normally remember, stuff that makes me look careless even though I’m not trying to be. And that’s embarrassing, because sales is kind of all about staying on top of people and details, and lately I feel like I’m either running on empty or distracted by how off my routine is.

And then HR got involved after I called the employee-assistance line, so now it feels more real, I guess. Like it’s not just me having a weird few weeks and needing to get it together. Other people are noticing that something’s wrong.

At home it’s not great either. The eating thing throws everything off — like if I don’t eat all day, I’m irritable and scattered, and then if I’m up late eating a bunch of takeout and snacks, I feel awful the next morning and the whole day starts badly. So yeah, work and home both feel like they’re slipping out of my control a little, even if I’m technically still showing up.

It’s been all over the place enough that I started thinking, okay, maybe I should actually talk to somebody instead of pretending it’s just a weird week.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of your eating when you're having those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, it’s not like I sit there planning it out, like “I’m going to eat all this food tonight.” It’s more like I’ll suddenly realize it’s after midnight and I’m just tearing through whatever’s there — takeout, snacks, stuff I bought earlier and forgot about — and then afterward I’m like, why did I do that?\n\nAnd then the weird part is other days are the total opposite. Yeah… during those late-night ones, it kind of feels that way. Not every single time like I’m completely out of control, but once it starts, it’s hard to just stop and be like, “Okay, that’s enough.”

I’m still showing up, but work and home both feel like they’re slipping out of my control.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 month\n  manifestation: This whole episode has been going on since the fight with my regional manager about a month ago.\n- key: depressive_episode_duration\n  name: Depressive Episode Duration\n  description: 4 months\n  manifestation: The low mood, guilt, and not caring about things have been around for about four months, and they got harder to ignore after the trouble at work.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 26 / sales_representative_wholesale_or_manufacturing / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S026\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you avoid eating certain foods because you're afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not really in the sense of, like, “I can’t eat bread” or “I’m avoiding dessert because I’m scared I’ll gain weight.” It hasn’t been that organized, I guess. Honestly, the thing that scared me into coming in is more that my eating has been all over the place.\n\nLike some days I’ll just forget meals completely, not because I’m trying to restrict or anything, but because I just don’t think about it or I’m distracted and then suddenly it’s late. And then other nights it goes the total opposite way, where after midnight I’m tearing through takeout and snacks, just eating whatever’s there. So it’s less about avoiding certain foods and more like I don’t feel like I have a normal pattern right now.\n\nDoctor: Has your irregular eating pattern been interfering with your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. I mean, I’m still functioning on the surface — I’m still getting to work, I’m still answering messages, still trying to keep things looking normal — but it’s starting to show in ways I can’t really explain away anymore.\n\nAt work, my manager has started pulling me off some accounts because I’m making mistakes I normally wouldn’t make. Like little details I’d usually catch, follow-ups I’d normally remember, stuff that makes me look careless even though I’m not trying to be. And that’s embarrassing, because sales is kind of all about staying on top of people and details, and lately I feel like I’m either running on empty or distracted by how off my routine is.\n\nAnd then HR got involved after I called the employee-assistance line, so now it feels more real, I guess. Like it’s not just me having a weird few weeks and needing to get it together. Other people are noticing that something’s wrong.\n\nAt home it’s not great either. The eating thing throws everything off — like if I don’t eat all day, I’m irritable and scattered, and then if I’m up late eating a bunch of takeout and snacks, I feel awful the next morning and the whole day starts badly. So yeah, work and home both feel like they’re slipping out of my control a little, even if I’m technically still showing up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating or control what you're eating during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… during those late-night ones, it kind of feels that way. Not every single time like I’m completely out of control, but once it starts, it’s hard to just stop and be like, “Okay, that’s enough.”\n\nIt’ll be after midnight, and I’ll realize I barely ate normally during the day, or I forgot meals altogether, and then suddenly I’m ordering takeout or grabbing snacks and just going through way more than I meant to. It’s not like I sit down with a plan. It’s more like I start eating and then I’m kind of on autopilot for a while.\n\nAnd that’s honestly the thing that scared me into coming in — not one specific food or the weight part as much as how all over the place it’s gotten. Some days I barely remember to eat, and then other nights I’m tearing through food like my body is trying to catch up all at once. So yeah, I guess I do feel like my control around it gets pretty shaky at night.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you feel like you're unable to control the amount of food you're eating during those late-night episodes?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, I think that’s the part that feels weirdest to me — not that I’m carefully deciding to eat a certain amount, but that once it’s late and I start, the amount kind of gets away from me.

Like, some days I’ll barely eat or I’ll forget meals completely, and then after midnight it’s like everything catches up with me at once. I’ll be ordering takeout or grabbing snacks, and it turns into more than I meant it to be. I’m not sitting there counting it or anything, but afterward I’m like, “Why did I eat all of that?” It just feels all over the place, like I don’t have a normal rhythm with food right now.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n9\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.4, F50.5\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Honestly, the thing that kind of pushed me to come in is my eating has been really off lately. It’s not even in one direction, which is why it’s been bothering me. Some days I’ll get to the end of the day and realize I barely ate anything, like I just forgot meals were a thing, and then other nights it’s the total opposite — I’m up late and suddenly I’m going through takeout, snacks, whatever’s around, way after midnight.\n\nIt’s embarrassing because it sounds simple, like “just eat normally,” but it hasn’t felt normal. It’s been all over the place enough that I started thinking, okay, maybe I should actually talk to somebody instead of pretending it’s just a weird week.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of your eating when you're having those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, it’s not like I sit there planning it out, like “I’m going to eat all this food tonight.” It’s more like I’ll suddenly realize it’s after midnight and I’m just tearing through whatever’s there — takeout, snacks, stuff I bought earlier and forgot about — and then afterward I’m like, why did I do that?\n\nAnd then the weird part is other days are the total opposite. I’ll go most of the day and it’s like meals don’t even register. I’ll look up and realize I basically forgot to eat. So the late-night stuff feels extra out of control because it’s not even consistent. It’s not just that I’m hungry at night — my eating in general has been all over the place, and that’s honestly what scared me enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after those late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it guilt exactly. Maybe embarrassed, yeah, because it feels kind of ridiculous afterward, like I should’ve been able to just stop or make a normal choice. But it’s not like I’m sitting there hating myself for it every single time.\n\nIt’s more this uncomfortable feeling of, “What was that?” Like I’ll look at the wrappers or the empty containers and feel kind of grossed out and annoyed with myself, but also confused, because in the moment it doesn’t feel that dramatic. Then afterward it feels like I wasn’t really paying attention to what I was doing. So… ashamed might be too strong, but embarrassed and frustrated, definitely.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really kept track on a scale, so I can’t say like, “I gained exactly this much” or “lost exactly this much.” But I wouldn’t be surprised if it’s changed some, because my eating has been so uneven.\n\nThat’s honestly the part that scared me into coming in — it’s not just a normal appetite shift. Some days I’ll realize I’ve basically skipped meals without meaning to, like I got through the whole day and food just didn’t occur to me. And then other nights it flips completely, and I’m up after midnight tearing through takeout and snacks like I can’t get a handle on it.\n\nSo weight-wise, I’m not sure of the number, but appetite-wise and eating pattern-wise, yeah, something has definitely been off.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever worry about gaining weight after these late-night eating episodes or because of your overall eating pattern?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess I worry a little, but it’s not exactly the weight part that’s been scaring me the most. Like, I haven’t been obsessing over “am I gaining weight” after every night or anything like that. It’s more that the whole pattern feels off and I don’t feel like I understand what my body is doing.\n\nBecause some days I’ll honestly just forget meals completely — like I’ll get through most of the day and then realize I didn’t really eat anything normal. And then other nights, especially after midnight, it’s like I’m suddenly tearing through takeout and snacks, just whatever’s around, and it feels way bigger than normal hunger.\n\nSo yeah, weight crosses my mind, like, “This probably can’t be good for me,” but the bigger worry is how all over the place it’s gotten. That’s the part that made me think I should actually talk to somebody instead of just assuming it’ll even out.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid eating certain foods because you're afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not really in the sense of, like, “I can’t eat bread” or “I’m avoiding dessert because I’m scared I’ll gain weight.” It hasn’t been that organized, I guess. Honestly, the thing that scared me into coming in is more that my eating has been all over the place.\n\nLike some days I’ll just forget meals completely, not because I’m trying to restrict or anything, but because I just don’t think about it or I’m distracted and then suddenly it’s late. And then other nights it goes the total opposite way, where after midnight I’m tearing through takeout and snacks, just eating whatever’s there. So it’s less about avoiding certain foods and more like I don’t feel like I have a normal pattern right now.\n\nDoctor: Has your irregular eating pattern been interfering with your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. I mean, I’m still functioning on the surface — I’m still getting to work, I’m still answering messages, still trying to keep things looking normal — but it’s starting to show in ways I can’t really explain away anymore.\n\nAt work, my manager has started pulling me off some accounts because I’m making mistakes I normally wouldn’t make. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I do. Probably more than I should.

After it happens, it’s not even just, like, “Oh, I ate too much, that was unhealthy.” It turns into this whole spiral where I start criticizing everything about myself. I’ll be sitting there feeling gross and wired and ashamed, and then my brain jumps straight to work — especially those Providence accounts. I keep replaying every mistake I made with them, like every missed detail, every follow-up I handled wrong, every little thing my manager probably noticed.

And then it gets really harsh in my head. I start thinking I’ve blown my whole future over this, like I’m useless, like everybody at work can see I’m falling apart or that I’m not as capable as they thought I was. It’s embarrassing to say out loud because part of me knows that sounds extreme, but in the moment it feels really true. Like the eating thing becomes proof that I can’t control anything, and then the work mistakes become proof that I’m just… failing, I guess.

I mean, it’s not like I sit there planning it out, like “I’m going to eat all this food tonight.” It’s more like I’ll suddenly realize it’s after midnight and I’m just tearing through whatever’s there — takeout, snacks, stuff I bought earlier and forgot about — and then afterward I’m like, why did I do that?\n\nAnd then the weird part is other days are the total opposite. I’ll go most of the day and it’s like meals don’t even register. I’ll look up and realize I basically forgot to eat. So the late-night stuff feels extra out of control because it’s not even consistent. It’s not just that I’m hungry at night — my eating in general has been all over the place, and that’s honestly what scared me enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after those late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it guilt exactly. Maybe embarrassed, yeah, because it feels kind of ridiculous afterward, like I should’ve been able to just stop or make a normal choice. But it’s not like I’m sitting there hating myself for it every single time.\n\nIt’s more this uncomfortable feeling of, “What was that?” Like I’ll look at the wrappers or the empty containers and feel kind of grossed out and annoyed with myself, but also confused, because in the moment it doesn’t feel that dramatic. Then afterward it feels like I wasn’t really paying attention to what I was doing. So… ashamed might be too strong, but embarrassed and frustrated, definitely.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really kept track on a scale, so I can’t say like, “I gained exactly this much” or “lost exactly this much.” But I wouldn’t be surprised if it’s changed some, because my eating has been so uneven.\n\nThat’s honestly the part that scared me into coming in — it’s not just a normal appetite shift. Some days I’ll realize I’ve basically skipped meals without meaning to, like I got through the whole day and food just didn’t occur to me. And then other nights it flips completely, and I’m up after midnight tearing through takeout and snacks like I can’t get a handle on it.\n\nSo weight-wise, I’m not sure of the number, but appetite-wise and eating pattern-wise, yeah, something has definitely been off.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever worry about gaining weight after these late-night eating episodes or because of your overall eating pattern?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess I worry a little, but it’s not exactly the weight part that’s been scaring me the most. Like, I haven’t been obsessing over “am I gaining weight” after every night or anything like that. It’s more that the whole pattern feels off and I don’t feel like I understand what my body is doing.\n\nBecause some days I’ll honestly just forget meals completely — like I’ll get through most of the day and then realize I didn’t really eat anything normal. And then other nights, especially after midnight, it’s like I’m suddenly tearing through takeout and snacks, just whatever’s around, and it feels way bigger than normal hunger.\n\nSo yeah, weight crosses my mind, like, “This probably can’t be good for me,” but the bigger worry is how all over the place it’s gotten. That’s the part that made me think I should actually talk to somebody instead of just assuming it’ll even out.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid eating certain foods because you're afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not really in the sense of, like, “I can’t eat bread” or “I’m avoiding dessert because I’m scared I’ll gain weight.” It hasn’t been that organized, I guess. Honestly, the thing that scared me into coming in is more that my eating has been all over the place.\n\nLike some days I’ll just forget meals completely, not because I’m trying to restrict or anything, but because I just don’t think about it or I’m distracted and then suddenly it’s late. And then other nights it goes the total opposite way, where after midnight I’m tearing through takeout and snacks, just eating whatever’s there. So it’s less about avoiding certain foods and more like I don’t feel like I have a normal pattern right now.\n\nDoctor: Has your irregular eating pattern been interfering with your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. I mean, I’m still functioning on the surface — I’m still getting to work, I’m still answering messages, still trying to keep things looking normal — but it’s starting to show in ways I can’t really explain away anymore.\n\nAt work, my manager has started pulling me off some accounts because I’m making mistakes I normally wouldn’t make. Like little details I’d usually catch, follow-ups I’d normally remember, stuff that makes me look careless even though I’m not trying to be. And that’s embarrassing, because sales is kind of all about staying on top of people and details, and lately I feel like I’m either running on empty or distracted by how off my routine is.\n\nAnd then HR got involved after I called the employee-assistance line, so now it feels more real, I guess. Like it’s not just me having a weird few weeks and needing to get it together. Other people are noticing that something’s wrong.\n\nAt home it’s not great either. The eating thing throws everything off — like if I don’t eat all day, I’m irritable and scattered, and then if I’m up late eating a bunch of takeout and snacks, I feel awful the next morning and the whole day starts badly. So yeah, work and home both feel like they’re slipping out of my control a little, even if I’m technically still showing up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating or control what you're eating during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… during those late-night ones, it kind of feels that way. Not every single time like I’m completely out of control, but once it starts, it’s hard to just stop and be like, “Okay, that’s enough.”\n\nIt’ll be after midnight, and I’ll realize I barely ate normally during the day, or I forgot meals altogether, and then suddenly I’m ordering takeout or grabbing snacks and just going through way more than I meant to. It’s not like I sit down with a plan. It’s more like I start eating and then I’m kind of on autopilot for a while.\n\nAnd that’s honestly the thing that scared me into coming in — not one specific food or the weight part as much as how all over the place it’s gotten. Some days I barely remember to eat, and then other nights I’m tearing through food like my body is trying to catch up all at once. So yeah, I guess I do feel like my control around it gets pretty shaky at night.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to control the amount of food you're eating during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think that’s the part that feels weirdest to me — not that I’m carefully deciding to eat a certain amount, but that once it’s late and I start, the amount kind of gets away from me.\n\nLike, some days I’ll barely eat or I’ll forget meals completely, and then after midnight it’s like everything catches up with me at once. I’ll be ordering takeout or grabbing snacks, and it turns into more than I meant it to be. I’m not sitting there counting it or anything, but afterward I’m like, “Why did I eat all of that?” It just feels all over the place, like I don’t have a normal rhythm with food right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you criticize yourself after these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I don’t know if it’s exactly fear, honestly. I don’t think I’m sitting there during the day thinking, “I can’t eat,” or at night thinking, “I’m going to gain weight.” It feels less organized than that.

The thing that really scared me into coming in is just how all over the place my eating has gotten. Like some days I’ll forget meals completely — not always on purpose, just the day gets away from me — and then other nights, especially after midnight, I’m tearing through takeout and snacks like I’m making up for the whole day at once.

So, I guess it’s more about not having any normal rhythm with it. It’s not really a clear fear of gaining weight or a clear fear of not eating enough. It’s just this swing between barely eating and then eating way too much late at night, and afterward I’m sitting there wondering what happened.

Like, I haven’t been obsessing over “am I gaining weight” after every night or anything like that. It’s more that the whole pattern feels off and I don’t feel like I understand what my body is doing.\n\nBecause some days I’ll honestly just forget meals completely — like I’ll get through most of the day and then realize I didn’t really eat anything normal. And then other nights, especially after midnight, it’s like I’m suddenly tearing through takeout and snacks, just whatever’s around, and it feels way bigger than normal hunger.\n\nSo yeah, weight crosses my mind, like, “This probably can’t be good for me,” but the bigger worry is how all over the place it’s gotten. That’s the part that made me think I should actually talk to somebody instead of just assuming it’ll even out.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid eating certain foods because you're afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not really in the sense of, like, “I can’t eat bread” or “I’m avoiding dessert because I’m scared I’ll gain weight.” It hasn’t been that organized, I guess. Honestly, the thing that scared me into coming in is more that my eating has been all over the place.\n\nLike some days I’ll just forget meals completely, not because I’m trying to restrict or anything, but because I just don’t think about it or I’m distracted and then suddenly it’s late. And then other nights it goes the total opposite way, where after midnight I’m tearing through takeout and snacks, just eating whatever’s there. So it’s less about avoiding certain foods and more like I don’t feel like I have a normal pattern right now.\n\nDoctor: Has your irregular eating pattern been interfering with your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. I mean, I’m still functioning on the surface — I’m still getting to work, I’m still answering messages, still trying to keep things looking normal — but it’s starting to show in ways I can’t really explain away anymore.\n\nAt work, my manager has started pulling me off some accounts because I’m making mistakes I normally wouldn’t make. Like little details I’d usually catch, follow-ups I’d normally remember, stuff that makes me look careless even though I’m not trying to be. And that’s embarrassing, because sales is kind of all about staying on top of people and details, and lately I feel like I’m either running on empty or distracted by how off my routine is.\n\nAnd then HR got involved after I called the employee-assistance line, so now it feels more real, I guess. Like it’s not just me having a weird few weeks and needing to get it together. Other people are noticing that something’s wrong.\n\nAt home it’s not great either. The eating thing throws everything off — like if I don’t eat all day, I’m irritable and scattered, and then if I’m up late eating a bunch of takeout and snacks, I feel awful the next morning and the whole day starts badly. So yeah, work and home both feel like they’re slipping out of my control a little, even if I’m technically still showing up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating or control what you're eating during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… during those late-night ones, it kind of feels that way. Not every single time like I’m completely out of control, but once it starts, it’s hard to just stop and be like, “Okay, that’s enough.”\n\nIt’ll be after midnight, and I’ll realize I barely ate normally during the day, or I forgot meals altogether, and then suddenly I’m ordering takeout or grabbing snacks and just going through way more than I meant to. It’s not like I sit down with a plan. It’s more like I start eating and then I’m kind of on autopilot for a while.\n\nAnd that’s honestly the thing that scared me into coming in — not one specific food or the weight part as much as how all over the place it’s gotten. Some days I barely remember to eat, and then other nights I’m tearing through food like my body is trying to catch up all at once. So yeah, I guess I do feel like my control around it gets pretty shaky at night.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to control the amount of food you're eating during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think that’s the part that feels weirdest to me — not that I’m carefully deciding to eat a certain amount, but that once it’s late and I start, the amount kind of gets away from me.\n\nLike, some days I’ll barely eat or I’ll forget meals completely, and then after midnight it’s like everything catches up with me at once. I’ll be ordering takeout or grabbing snacks, and it turns into more than I meant it to be. I’m not sitting there counting it or anything, but afterward I’m like, “Why did I eat all of that?” It just feels all over the place, like I don’t have a normal rhythm with food right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you criticize yourself after these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. Probably more than I should.\n\nAfter it happens, it’s not even just, like, “Oh, I ate too much, that was unhealthy.” It turns into this whole spiral where I start criticizing everything about myself. I’ll be sitting there feeling gross and wired and ashamed, and then my brain jumps straight to work — especially those Providence accounts. I keep replaying every mistake I made with them, like every missed detail, every follow-up I handled wrong, every little thing my manager probably noticed.\n\nAnd then it gets really harsh in my head. I start thinking I’ve blown my whole future over this, like I’m useless, like everybody at work can see I’m falling apart or that I’m not as capable as they thought I was. It’s embarrassing to say out loud because part of me knows that sounds extreme, but in the moment it feels really true. Like the eating thing becomes proof that I can’t control anything, and then the work mistakes become proof that I’m just… failing, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your late-night eating episodes are related to a fear of not eating enough during the day or a fear of gaining weight from eating too much at night?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s exactly fear, honestly. I don’t think I’m sitting there during the day thinking, “I can’t eat,” or at night thinking, “I’m going to gain weight.” It feels less organized than that.\n\nThe thing that really scared me into coming in is just how all over the place my eating has gotten. Like some days I’ll forget meals completely — not always on purpose, just the day gets away from me — and then other nights, especially after midnight, I’m tearing through takeout and snacks like I’m making up for the whole day at once.\n\nSo, I guess it’s more about not having any normal rhythm with it. It’s not really a clear fear of gaining weight or a clear fear of not eating enough. It’s just this swing between barely eating and then eating way too much late at night, and afterward I’m sitting there wondering what happened.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It’s embarrassing to say out loud because part of me knows that sounds extreme, but in the moment it feels really true. Like the eating thing becomes proof that I can’t control anything, and then the work mistakes become proof that I’m just… failing, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your late-night eating episodes are related to a fear of not eating enough during the day or a fear of gaining weight from eating too much at night?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s exactly fear, honestly. I don’t think I’m sitting there during the day thinking, “I can’t eat,” or at night thinking, “I’m going to gain weight.” It feels less organized than that.\n\nThe thing that really scared me into coming in is just how all over the place my eating has gotten. Like some days I’ll forget meals completely — not always on purpose, just the day gets away from me — and then other nights, especially after midnight, I’m tearing through takeout and snacks like I’m making up for the whole day at once.\n\nSo, I guess it’s more about not having any normal rhythm with it. It’s not really a clear fear of gaining weight or a clear fear of not eating enough. It’s just this swing between barely eating and then eating way too much late at night, and afterward I’m sitting there wondering what happened.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think your eating patterns are influenced by a desire to avoid gaining weight?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 26 / sales_representative_wholesale_or_manufacturing / high_school\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Weight_Appetite_Change\nmanifestation: \"Honestly, the thing that scared me into coming in is my eating has been all over the place, like some days I forget meals completely and other nights I'm tearing through takeout and snacks after midnight.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not really, no — at least I don’t think that’s the main thing driving it.

It’s not like I’m spending the day trying not to eat because I’m worried about weight, or then at night I’m thinking about my body or calories or anything like that. Honestly, the thing that scared me into coming in is just how all over the place my eating has been. Some days I’ll forget meals completely, like I’ll realize way later that I haven’t really eaten, and then other nights I’m tearing through takeout and snacks after midnight like I can’t get a normal handle on it.

So if weight is in my head at all, it’s not the main feeling. The main thing is that the pattern feels really off and out of control — like there’s no normal rhythm to it.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n12\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.4, F50.5\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Honestly, the thing that kind of pushed me to come in is my eating has been really off lately. It’s not even in one direction, which is why it’s been bothering me. Some days I’ll get to the end of the day and realize I barely ate anything, like I just forgot meals were a thing, and then other nights it’s the total opposite — I’m up late and suddenly I’m going through takeout, snacks, whatever’s around, way after midnight.\n\nIt’s embarrassing because it sounds simple, like “just eat normally,” but it hasn’t felt normal. It’s been all over the place enough that I started thinking, okay, maybe I should actually talk to somebody instead of pretending it’s just a weird week.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of your eating when you're having those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, it’s not like I sit there planning it out, like “I’m going to eat all this food tonight.” It’s more like I’ll suddenly realize it’s after midnight and I’m just tearing through whatever’s there — takeout, snacks, stuff I bought earlier and forgot about — and then afterward I’m like, why did I do that?\n\nAnd then the weird part is other days are the total opposite. I’ll go most of the day and it’s like meals don’t even register. I’ll look up and realize I basically forgot to eat. So the late-night stuff feels extra out of control because it’s not even consistent. It’s not just that I’m hungry at night — my eating in general has been all over the place, and that’s honestly what scared me enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after those late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it guilt exactly. Maybe embarrassed, yeah, because it feels kind of ridiculous afterward, like I should’ve been able to just stop or make a normal choice. But it’s not like I’m sitting there hating myself for it every single time.\n\nIt’s more this uncomfortable feeling of, “What was that?” Like I’ll look at the wrappers or the empty containers and feel kind of grossed out and annoyed with myself, but also confused, because in the moment it doesn’t feel that dramatic. Then afterward it feels like I wasn’t really paying attention to what I was doing. So… ashamed might be too strong, but embarrassed and frustrated, definitely.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really kept track on a scale, so I can’t say like, “I gained exactly this much” or “lost exactly this much.” But I wouldn’t be surprised if it’s changed some, because my eating has been so uneven.\n\nThat’s honestly the part that scared me into coming in — it’s not just a normal appetite shift. Some days I’ll realize I’ve basically skipped meals without meaning to, like I got through the whole day and food just didn’t occur to me. And then other nights it flips completely, and I’m up after midnight tearing through takeout and snacks like I can’t get a handle on it.\n\nSo weight-wise, I’m not sure of the number, but appetite-wise and eating pattern-wise, yeah, something has definitely been off.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever worry about gaining weight after these late-night eating episodes or because of your overall eating pattern?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess I worry a little, but it’s not exactly the weight part that’s been scaring me the most. Like, I haven’t been obsessing over “am I gaining weight” after every night or anything like that. It’s more that the whole pattern feels off and I don’t feel like I understand what my body is doing.\n\nBecause some days I’ll honestly just forget meals completely — like I’ll get through most of the day and then realize I didn’t really eat anything normal. And then other nights, especially after midnight, it’s like I’m suddenly tearing through takeout and snacks, just whatever’s around, and it feels way bigger than normal hunger.\n\nSo yeah, weight crosses my mind, like, “This probably can’t be good for me,” but the bigger worry is how all over the place it’s gotten. That’s the part that made me think I should actually talk to somebody instead of just assuming it’ll even out.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid eating certain foods because you're afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not really in the sense of, like, “I can’t eat bread” or “I’m avoiding dessert because I’m scared I’ll gain weight.” It hasn’t been that organized, I guess. Honestly, the thing that scared me into coming in is more that my eating has been all over the place.\n\nLike some days I’ll just forget meals completely, not because I’m trying to restrict or anything, but because I just don’t think about it or I’m distracted and then suddenly it’s late. And then other nights it goes the total opposite way, where after midnight I’m tearing through takeout and snacks, just eating whatever’s there. So it’s less about avoiding certain foods and more like I don’t feel like I have a normal pattern right now.\n\nDoctor: Has your irregular eating pattern been interfering with your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. I mean, I’m still functioning on the surface — I’m still getting to work, I’m still answering messages, still trying to keep things looking normal — but it’s starting to show in ways I can’t really explain away anymore.\n\nAt work, my manager has started pulling me off some accounts because I’m making mistakes I normally wouldn’t make. Like little details I’d usually catch, follow-ups I’d normally remember, stuff that makes me look careless even though I’m not trying to be. And that’s embarrassing, because sales is kind of all about staying on top of people and details, and lately I feel like I’m either running on empty or distracted by how off my routine is.\n\nAnd then HR got involved after I called the employee-assistance line, so now it feels more real, I guess. Like it’s not just me having a weird few weeks and needing to get it together. Other people are noticing that something’s wrong.\n\nAt home it’s not great either. The eating thing throws everything off — like if I don’t eat all day, I’m irritable and scattered, and then if I’m up late eating a bunch of takeout and snacks, I feel awful the next morning and the whole day starts badly. So yeah, work and home both feel like they’re slipping out of my control a little, even if I’m technically still showing up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating or control what you're eating during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… during those late-night ones, it kind of feels that way. Not every single time like I’m completely out of control, but once it starts, it’s hard to just stop and be like, “Okay, that’s enough.”\n\nIt’ll be after midnight, and I’ll realize I barely ate normally during the day, or I forgot meals altogether, and then suddenly I’m ordering takeout or grabbing snacks and just going through way more than I meant to. It’s not like I sit down with a plan. It’s more like I start eating and then I’m kind of on autopilot for a while.\n\nAnd that’s honestly the thing that scared me into coming in — not one specific food or the weight part as much as how all over the place it’s gotten. Some days I barely remember to eat, and then other nights I’m tearing through food like my body is trying to catch up all at once. So yeah, I guess I do feel like my control around it gets pretty shaky at night.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to control the amount of food you're eating during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think that’s the part that feels weirdest to me — not that I’m carefully deciding to eat a certain amount, but that once it’s late and I start, the amount kind of gets away from me.\n\nLike, some days I’ll barely eat or I’ll forget meals completely, and then after midnight it’s like everything catches up with me at once. I’ll be ordering takeout or grabbing snacks, and it turns into more than I meant it to be. I’m not sitting there counting it or anything, but afterward I’m like, “Why did I eat all of that?” It just feels all over the place, like I don’t have a normal rhythm with food right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you criticize yourself after these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. Yeah, I think sometimes that’s part of it, but it doesn’t feel like the whole explanation.

Like little details I’d usually catch, follow-ups I’d normally remember, stuff that makes me look careless even though I’m not trying to be. I mean, it’s not like I sit there planning it out, like “I’m going to eat all this food tonight.” It’s more like I’ll suddenly realize it’s after midnight and I’m just tearing through whatever’s there — takeout, snacks, stuff I bought earlier and forgot about — and then afterward I’m like, why did I do that?\n\nAnd then the weird part is other days are the total opposite. I’ll go most of the day and it’s like meals don’t even register. I’ll look up and realize I basically forgot to eat. So the late-night stuff feels extra out of control because it’s not even consistent. It’s not just that I’m hungry at night — my eating in general has been all over the place, and that’s honestly what scared me enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after those late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it guilt exactly. Maybe embarrassed, yeah, because it feels kind of ridiculous afterward, like I should’ve been able to just stop or make a normal choice. But it’s not like I’m sitting there hating myself for it every single time.\n\nIt’s more this uncomfortable feeling of, “What was that?” Like I’ll look at the wrappers or the empty containers and feel kind of grossed out and annoyed with myself, but also confused, because in the moment it doesn’t feel that dramatic. Then afterward it feels like I wasn’t really paying attention to what I was doing. So… ashamed might be too strong, but embarrassed and frustrated, definitely.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really kept track on a scale, so I can’t say like, “I gained exactly this much” or “lost exactly this much.” But I wouldn’t be surprised if it’s changed some, because my eating has been so uneven.\n\nThat’s honestly the part that scared me into coming in — it’s not just a normal appetite shift. Some days I’ll realize I’ve basically skipped meals without meaning to, like I got through the whole day and food just didn’t occur to me. And then other nights it flips completely, and I’m up after midnight tearing through takeout and snacks like I can’t get a handle on it.\n\nSo weight-wise, I’m not sure of the number, but appetite-wise and eating pattern-wise, yeah, something has definitely been off.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever worry about gaining weight after these late-night eating episodes or because of your overall eating pattern?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess I worry a little, but it’s not exactly the weight part that’s been scaring me the most. Like, I haven’t been obsessing over “am I gaining weight” after every night or anything like that. It’s more that the whole pattern feels off and I don’t feel like I understand what my body is doing.\n\nBecause some days I’ll honestly just forget meals completely — like I’ll get through most of the day and then realize I didn’t really eat anything normal. And then other nights, especially after midnight, it’s like I’m suddenly tearing through takeout and snacks, just whatever’s around, and it feels way bigger than normal hunger.\n\nSo yeah, weight crosses my mind, like, “This probably can’t be good for me,” but the bigger worry is how all over the place it’s gotten. That’s the part that made me think I should actually talk to somebody instead of just assuming it’ll even out.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid eating certain foods because you're afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not really in the sense of, like, “I can’t eat bread” or “I’m avoiding dessert because I’m scared I’ll gain weight.” It hasn’t been that organized, I guess. Honestly, the thing that scared me into coming in is more that my eating has been all over the place.\n\nLike some days I’ll just forget meals completely, not because I’m trying to restrict or anything, but because I just don’t think about it or I’m distracted and then suddenly it’s late. And then other nights it goes the total opposite way, where after midnight I’m tearing through takeout and snacks, just eating whatever’s there. So it’s less about avoiding certain foods and more like I don’t feel like I have a normal pattern right now.\n\nDoctor: Has your irregular eating pattern been interfering with your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. I mean, I’m still functioning on the surface — I’m still getting to work, I’m still answering messages, still trying to keep things looking normal — but it’s starting to show in ways I can’t really explain away anymore.\n\nAt work, my manager has started pulling me off some accounts because I’m making mistakes I normally wouldn’t make. Like little details I’d usually catch, follow-ups I’d normally remember, stuff that makes me look careless even though I’m not trying to be. And that’s embarrassing, because sales is kind of all about staying on top of people and details, and lately I feel like I’m either running on empty or distracted by how off my routine is.\n\nAnd then HR got involved after I called the employee-assistance line, so now it feels more real, I guess. Like it’s not just me having a weird few weeks and needing to get it together. Other people are noticing that something’s wrong.\n\nAt home it’s not great either. The eating thing throws everything off — like if I don’t eat all day, I’m irritable and scattered, and then if I’m up late eating a bunch of takeout and snacks, I feel awful the next morning and the whole day starts badly. So yeah, work and home both feel like they’re slipping out of my control a little, even if I’m technically still showing up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating or control what you're eating during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… during those late-night ones, it kind of feels that way. Not every single time like I’m completely out of control, but once it starts, it’s hard to just stop and be like, “Okay, that’s enough.”\n\nIt’ll be after midnight, and I’ll realize I barely ate normally during the day, or I forgot meals altogether, and then suddenly I’m ordering takeout or grabbing snacks and just going through way more than I meant to. It’s not like I sit down with a plan. It’s more like I start eating and then I’m kind of on autopilot for a while.\n\nAnd that’s honestly the thing that scared me into coming in — not one specific food or the weight part as much as how all over the place it’s gotten. Some days I barely remember to eat, and then other nights I’m tearing through food like my body is trying to catch up all at once. So yeah, I guess I do feel like my control around it gets pretty shaky at night.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to control the amount of food you're eating during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think that’s the part that feels weirdest to me — not that I’m carefully deciding to eat a certain amount, but that once it’s late and I start, the amount kind of gets away from me.\n\nLike, some days I’ll barely eat or I’ll forget meals completely, and then after midnight it’s like everything catches up with me at once. I’ll be ordering takeout or grabbing snacks, and it turns into more than I meant it to be. I’m not sitting there counting it or anything, but afterward I’m like, “Why did I eat all of that?” It just feels all over the place, like I don’t have a normal rhythm with food right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you criticize yourself after these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. Probably more than I should.\n\nAfter it happens, it’s not even just, like, “Oh, I ate too much, that was unhealthy.” It turns into this whole spiral where I start criticizing everything about myself. I’ll be sitting there feeling gross and wired and ashamed, and then my brain jumps straight to work — especially those Providence accounts. I keep replaying every mistake I made with them, like every missed detail, every follow-up I handled wrong, every little thing my manager probably noticed.\n\nAnd then it gets really harsh in my head. I start thinking I’ve blown my whole future over this, like I’m useless, like everybody at work can see I’m falling apart or that I’m not as capable as they thought I was. It’s embarrassing to say out loud because part of me knows that sounds extreme, but in the moment it feels really true. Like some days I’ll forget meals completely — not always on purpose, just the day gets away from me — and then other nights, especially after midnight, I’m tearing through takeout and snacks like I’m making up for the whole day at once.\n\nSo, I guess it’s more about not having any normal rhythm with it. It’s not really a clear fear of gaining weight or a clear fear of not eating enough. It’s just this swing between barely eating and then eating way too much late at night, and afterward I’m sitting there wondering what happened.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating patterns are influenced by a desire to avoid gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not really, no — at least I don’t think that’s the main thing driving it.\n\nIt’s not like I’m spending the day trying not to eat because I’m worried about weight, or then at night I’m thinking about my body or calories or anything like that. Honestly, the thing that scared me into coming in is just how all over the place my eating has been. Some days I’ll forget meals completely, like I’ll realize way later that I haven’t really eaten, and then other nights I’m tearing through takeout and snacks after midnight like I can’t get a normal handle on it.\n\nSo if weight is in my head at all, it’s not the main feeling. I mean… yeah, sometimes it probably is making up for not eating during the day, but I don’t know if I’d say that’s the whole reason.

The bigger thing, honestly, and I know I’ve kind of said this already, is that my eating has just been all over the place. Then other nights, especially after midnight, I’m suddenly tearing through takeout and snacks like I’m trying to catch up all at once.

It doesn’t really feel like I’m worried about some specific thing in that moment. It feels more like I lose the rhythm of eating normally, and then at night it just kind of spills over. Afterward I’m like, “Why did I eat all that?” but at the time it feels hard to slow down.

Some days I’ll get to the end of the day and realize I barely ate anything, like I just forgot meals were a thing, and then other nights it’s the total opposite — I’m up late and suddenly I’m going through takeout, snacks, whatever’s around, way after midnight.\n\nIt’s embarrassing because it sounds simple, like “just eat normally,” but it hasn’t felt normal. It’s been all over the place enough that I started thinking, okay, maybe I should actually talk to somebody instead of pretending it’s just a weird week.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of your eating when you're having those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, it’s not like I sit there planning it out, like “I’m going to eat all this food tonight.” It’s more like I’ll suddenly realize it’s after midnight and I’m just tearing through whatever’s there — takeout, snacks, stuff I bought earlier and forgot about — and then afterward I’m like, why did I do that?\n\nAnd then the weird part is other days are the total opposite. I’ll go most of the day and it’s like meals don’t even register. I’ll look up and realize I basically forgot to eat. So the late-night stuff feels extra out of control because it’s not even consistent. It’s not just that I’m hungry at night — my eating in general has been all over the place, and that’s honestly what scared me enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after those late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it guilt exactly. Maybe embarrassed, yeah, because it feels kind of ridiculous afterward, like I should’ve been able to just stop or make a normal choice. But it’s not like I’m sitting there hating myself for it every single time.\n\nIt’s more this uncomfortable feeling of, “What was that?” Like I’ll look at the wrappers or the empty containers and feel kind of grossed out and annoyed with myself, but also confused, because in the moment it doesn’t feel that dramatic. Then afterward it feels like I wasn’t really paying attention to what I was doing. So… ashamed might be too strong, but embarrassed and frustrated, definitely.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really kept track on a scale, so I can’t say like, “I gained exactly this much” or “lost exactly this much.” But I wouldn’t be surprised if it’s changed some, because my eating has been so uneven.\n\nThat’s honestly the part that scared me into coming in — it’s not just a normal appetite shift. Some days I’ll realize I’ve basically skipped meals without meaning to, like I got through the whole day and food just didn’t occur to me. And then other nights it flips completely, and I’m up after midnight tearing through takeout and snacks like I can’t get a handle on it.\n\nSo weight-wise, I’m not sure of the number, but appetite-wise and eating pattern-wise, yeah, something has definitely been off.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever worry about gaining weight after these late-night eating episodes or because of your overall eating pattern?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess I worry a little, but it’s not exactly the weight part that’s been scaring me the most. Like, I haven’t been obsessing over “am I gaining weight” after every night or anything like that. It’s more that the whole pattern feels off and I don’t feel like I understand what my body is doing.\n\nBecause some days I’ll honestly just forget meals completely — like I’ll get through most of the day and then realize I didn’t really eat anything normal. And then other nights, especially after midnight, it’s like I’m suddenly tearing through takeout and snacks, just whatever’s around, and it feels way bigger than normal hunger.\n\nSo yeah, weight crosses my mind, like, “This probably can’t be good for me,” but the bigger worry is how all over the place it’s gotten. That’s the part that made me think I should actually talk to somebody instead of just assuming it’ll even out.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid eating certain foods because you're afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not really in the sense of, like, “I can’t eat bread” or “I’m avoiding dessert because I’m scared I’ll gain weight.” It hasn’t been that organized, I guess. Honestly, the thing that scared me into coming in is more that my eating has been all over the place.\n\nLike some days I’ll just forget meals completely, not because I’m trying to restrict or anything, but because I just don’t think about it or I’m distracted and then suddenly it’s late. And then other nights it goes the total opposite way, where after midnight I’m tearing through takeout and snacks, just eating whatever’s there. So it’s less about avoiding certain foods and more like I don’t feel like I have a normal pattern right now.\n\nDoctor: Has your irregular eating pattern been interfering with your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. I mean, I’m still functioning on the surface — I’m still getting to work, I’m still answering messages, still trying to keep things looking normal — but it’s starting to show in ways I can’t really explain away anymore.\n\nAt work, my manager has started pulling me off some accounts because I’m making mistakes I normally wouldn’t make. Like little details I’d usually catch, follow-ups I’d normally remember, stuff that makes me look careless even though I’m not trying to be. And that’s embarrassing, because sales is kind of all about staying on top of people and details, and lately I feel like I’m either running on empty or distracted by how off my routine is.\n\nAnd then HR got involved after I called the employee-assistance line, so now it feels more real, I guess. Like it’s not just me having a weird few weeks and needing to get it together. Other people are noticing that something’s wrong.\n\nAt home it’s not great either. The eating thing throws everything off — like if I don’t eat all day, I’m irritable and scattered, and then if I’m up late eating a bunch of takeout and snacks, I feel awful the next morning and the whole day starts badly. So yeah, work and home both feel like they’re slipping out of my control a little, even if I’m technically still showing up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating or control what you're eating during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… during those late-night ones, it kind of feels that way. Not every single time like I’m completely out of control, but once it starts, it’s hard to just stop and be like, “Okay, that’s enough.”\n\nIt’ll be after midnight, and I’ll realize I barely ate normally during the day, or I forgot meals altogether, and then suddenly I’m ordering takeout or grabbing snacks and just going through way more than I meant to. It’s not like I sit down with a plan. It’s more like I start eating and then I’m kind of on autopilot for a while.\n\nAnd that’s honestly the thing that scared me into coming in — not one specific food or the weight part as much as how all over the place it’s gotten. Some days I barely remember to eat, and then other nights I’m tearing through food like my body is trying to catch up all at once. So yeah, I guess I do feel like my control around it gets pretty shaky at night.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to control the amount of food you're eating during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think that’s the part that feels weirdest to me — not that I’m carefully deciding to eat a certain amount, but that once it’s late and I start, the amount kind of gets away from me.\n\nLike, some days I’ll barely eat or I’ll forget meals completely, and then after midnight it’s like everything catches up with me at once. I’ll be ordering takeout or grabbing snacks, and it turns into more than I meant it to be. I’m not sitting there counting it or anything, but afterward I’m like, “Why did I eat all of that?” It just feels all over the place, like I don’t have a normal rhythm with food right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you criticize yourself after these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. Probably more than I should.\n\nAfter it happens, it’s not even just, like, “Oh, I ate too much, that was unhealthy.” It turns into this whole spiral where I start criticizing everything about myself. I’ll be sitting there feeling gross and wired and ashamed, and then my brain jumps straight to work — especially those Providence accounts. I keep replaying every mistake I made with them, like every missed detail, every follow-up I handled wrong, every little thing my manager probably noticed.\n\nAnd then it gets really harsh in my head. I start thinking I’ve blown my whole future over this, like I’m useless, like everybody at work can see I’m falling apart or that I’m not as capable as they thought I was. It’s embarrassing to say out loud because part of me knows that sounds extreme, but in the moment it feels really true. Like the eating thing becomes proof that I can’t control anything, and then the work mistakes become proof that I’m just… failing, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your late-night eating episodes are related to a fear of not eating enough during the day or a fear of gaining weight from eating too much at night?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s exactly fear, honestly. I don’t think I’m sitting there during the day thinking, “I can’t eat,” or at night thinking, “I’m going to gain weight.” It feels less organized than that.\n\nThe thing that really scared me into coming in is just how all over the place my eating has gotten. Like some days I’ll forget meals completely — not always on purpose, just the day gets away from me — and then other nights, especially after midnight, I’m tearing through takeout and snacks like I’m making up for the whole day at once.\n\nSo, I guess it’s more about not having any normal rhythm with it. It’s not really a clear fear of gaining weight or a clear fear of not eating enough. It’s just this swing between barely eating and then eating way too much late at night, and afterward I’m sitting there wondering what happened.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating patterns are influenced by a desire to avoid gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not really, no — at least I don’t think that’s the main thing driving it.\n\nIt’s not like I’m spending the day trying not to eat because I’m worried about weight, or then at night I’m thinking about my body or calories or anything like that. Honestly, the thing that scared me into coming in is just how all over the place my eating has been. Some days I’ll forget meals completely, like I’ll realize way later that I haven’t really eaten, and then other nights I’m tearing through takeout and snacks after midnight like I can’t get a normal handle on it.\n\nSo if weight is in my head at all, it’s not the main feeling. The main thing is that the pattern feels really off and out of control — like there’s no normal rhythm to it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your late-night eating episodes are a result of feeling hungry after not eating enough during the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think sometimes that’s part of it, but it doesn’t feel like the whole explanation.\n\nLike, there are definitely days where I’ll look back and realize I barely ate anything normal during the day — not because I had some plan, just because I got busy or distracted or whatever — and then by the time it’s late, especially after midnight, it’s like my body and brain both catch up at once. Then I’m ordering takeout or going through snacks way more than I meant to.\n\nBut honestly, the thing that scared me into coming in is more that my eating has been all over the place. Some days I forget meals completely, and then other nights I’m tearing through food like I’m trying to make up for it, but it doesn’t feel calm or normal. It feels kind of out of rhythm, like I can’t get a steady pattern going.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the reason you eat so much at night is because you're trying to make up for not eating during the day or is it because you're worried about something else?\n\nPatient: I mean… yeah, sometimes it probably is making up for not eating during the day, but I don’t know if I’d say that’s the whole reason.\n\nThe bigger thing, honestly, and I know I’ve kind of said this already, is that my eating has just been all over the place. Some days I’ll go most of the day and realize I forgot meals completely, like I didn’t sit down and eat anything normal. Then other nights, especially after midnight, I’m suddenly tearing through takeout and snacks like I’m trying to catch up all at once.\n\nIt doesn’t really feel like I’m worried about some specific thing in that moment. It feels more like I lose the rhythm of eating normally, and then at night it just kind of spills over. Afterward I’m like, “Why did I eat all that?” but at the time it feels hard to slow down.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

No, I really don’t think it’s about fear of gaining weight. Like, I’m not sitting there thinking, “I can’t eat because I’ll get bigger,” or checking calories or anything like that.

It’s been all over the place enough that I started thinking, okay, maybe I should actually talk to somebody instead of pretending it’s just a weird week.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of your eating when you're having those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, it’s not like I sit there planning it out, like “I’m going to eat all this food tonight.” It’s more like I’ll suddenly realize it’s after midnight and I’m just tearing through whatever’s there — takeout, snacks, stuff I bought earlier and forgot about — and then afterward I’m like, why did I do that?\n\nAnd then the weird part is other days are the total opposite. Maybe embarrassed, yeah, because it feels kind of ridiculous afterward, like I should’ve been able to just stop or make a normal choice. Then afterward it feels like I wasn’t really paying attention to what I was doing. Some days I’ll realize I’ve basically skipped meals without meaning to, like I got through the whole day and food just didn’t occur to me. That’s the part that made me think I should actually talk to somebody instead of just assuming it’ll even out.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid eating certain foods because you're afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not really in the sense of, like, “I can’t eat bread” or “I’m avoiding dessert because I’m scared I’ll gain weight.” It hasn’t been that organized, I guess. So it’s less about avoiding certain foods and more like I don’t feel like I have a normal pattern right now.\n\nDoctor: Has your irregular eating pattern been interfering with your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. I mean, I’m still functioning on the surface — I’m still getting to work, I’m still answering messages, still trying to keep things looking normal — but it’s starting to show in ways I can’t really explain away anymore.\n\nAt work, my manager has started pulling me off some accounts because I’m making mistakes I normally wouldn’t make. Like little details I’d usually catch, follow-ups I’d normally remember, stuff that makes me look careless even though I’m not trying to be. And that’s embarrassing, because sales is kind of all about staying on top of people and details, and lately I feel like I’m either running on empty or distracted by how off my routine is.\n\nAnd then HR got involved after I called the employee-assistance line, so now it feels more real, I guess. Like it’s not just me having a weird few weeks and needing to get it together. Other people are noticing that something’s wrong.\n\nAt home it’s not great either. The eating thing throws everything off — like if I don’t eat all day, I’m irritable and scattered, and then if I’m up late eating a bunch of takeout and snacks, I feel awful the next morning and the whole day starts badly. So yeah, work and home both feel like they’re slipping out of my control a little, even if I’m technically still showing up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating or control what you're eating during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… during those late-night ones, it kind of feels that way. Not every single time like I’m completely out of control, but once it starts, it’s hard to just stop and be like, “Okay, that’s enough.”\n\nIt’ll be after midnight, and I’ll realize I barely ate normally during the day, or I forgot meals altogether, and then suddenly I’m ordering takeout or grabbing snacks and just going through way more than I meant to. It’s not like I sit down with a plan. It’s more like I start eating and then I’m kind of on autopilot for a while.\n\nAnd that’s honestly the thing that scared me into coming in — not one specific food or the weight part as much as how all over the place it’s gotten. Some days I barely remember to eat, and then other nights I’m tearing through food like my body is trying to catch up all at once. So yeah, I guess I do feel like my control around it gets pretty shaky at night.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to control the amount of food you're eating during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think that’s the part that feels weirdest to me — not that I’m carefully deciding to eat a certain amount, but that once it’s late and I start, the amount kind of gets away from me.\n\nLike, some days I’ll barely eat or I’ll forget meals completely, and then after midnight it’s like everything catches up with me at once. I’ll be ordering takeout or grabbing snacks, and it turns into more than I meant it to be. I’m not sitting there counting it or anything, but afterward I’m like, “Why did I eat all of that?” It just feels all over the place, like I don’t have a normal rhythm with food right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you criticize yourself after these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. Probably more than I should.\n\nAfter it happens, it’s not even just, like, “Oh, I ate too much, that was unhealthy.” It turns into this whole spiral where I start criticizing everything about myself.
