But it’s not like I’m sitting there hating myself for it every single time.\n\nIt’s more this uncomfortable feeling of, “What was that?” Like I’ll look at the wrappers or the empty containers and feel kind of grossed out and annoyed with myself, but also confused, because in the moment it doesn’t feel that dramatic. Then afterward it feels like I wasn’t really paying attention to what I was doing. So… ashamed might be too strong, but embarrassed and frustrated, definitely.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really kept track on a scale, so I can’t say like, “I gained exactly this much” or “lost exactly this much.” But I wouldn’t be surprised if it’s changed some, because my eating has been so uneven.\n\nThat’s honestly the part that scared me into coming in — it’s not just a normal appetite shift. Some days I’ll realize I’ve basically skipped meals without meaning to, like I got through the whole day and food just didn’t occur to me. And then other nights it flips completely, and I’m up after midnight tearing through takeout and snacks like I can’t get a handle on it.\n\nSo weight-wise, I’m not sure of the number, but appetite-wise and eating pattern-wise, yeah, something has definitely been off.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever worry about gaining weight after these late-night eating episodes or because of your overall eating pattern?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess I worry a little, but it’s not exactly the weight part that’s been scaring me the most. Like, I haven’t been obsessing over “am I gaining weight” after every night or anything like that. It’s more that the whole pattern feels off and I don’t feel like I understand what my body is doing.\n\nBecause some days I’ll honestly just forget meals completely — like I’ll get through most of the day and then realize I didn’t really eat anything normal. And then other nights, especially after midnight, it’s like I’m suddenly tearing through takeout and snacks, just whatever’s around, and it feels way bigger than normal hunger.\n\nSo yeah, weight crosses my mind, like, “This probably can’t be good for me,” but the bigger worry is how all over the place it’s gotten. That’s the part that made me think I should actually talk to somebody instead of just assuming it’ll even out.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid eating certain foods because you're afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not really in the sense of, like, “I can’t eat bread” or “I’m avoiding dessert because I’m scared I’ll gain weight.” It hasn’t been that organized, I guess. Honestly, the thing that scared me into coming in is more that my eating has been all over the place.\n\nLike some days I’ll just forget meals completely, not because I’m trying to restrict or anything, but because I just don’t think about it or I’m distracted and then suddenly it’s late. And then other nights it goes the total opposite way, where after midnight I’m tearing through takeout and snacks, just eating whatever’s there. So it’s less about avoiding certain foods and more like I don’t feel like I have a normal pattern right now.\n\nDoctor: Has your irregular eating pattern been interfering with your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. I mean, I’m still functioning on the surface — I’m still getting to work, I’m still answering messages, still trying to keep things looking normal — but it’s starting to show in ways I can’t really explain away anymore.\n\nAt work, my manager has started pulling me off some accounts because I’m making mistakes I normally wouldn’t make. Like little details I’d usually catch, follow-ups I’d normally remember, stuff that makes me look careless even though I’m not trying to be. And that’s embarrassing, because sales is kind of all about staying on top of people and details, and lately I feel like I’m either running on empty or distracted by how off my routine is.\n\nAnd then HR got involved after I called the employee-assistance line, so now it feels more real, I guess. Like it’s not just me having a weird few weeks and needing to get it together. Other people are noticing that something’s wrong.\n\nAt home it’s not great either. The eating thing throws everything off — like if I don’t eat all day, I’m irritable and scattered, and then if I’m up late eating a bunch of takeout and snacks, I feel awful the next morning and the whole day starts badly. So yeah, work and home both feel like they’re slipping out of my control a little, even if I’m technically still showing up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating or control what you're eating during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… during those late-night ones, it kind of feels that way. Not every single time like I’m completely out of control, but once it starts, it’s hard to just stop and be like, “Okay, that’s enough.”\n\nIt’ll be after midnight, and I’ll realize I barely ate normally during the day, or I forgot meals altogether, and then suddenly I’m ordering takeout or grabbing snacks and just going through way more than I meant to. It’s not like I sit down with a plan. It’s more like I start eating and then I’m kind of on autopilot for a while.\n\nAnd that’s honestly the thing that scared me into coming in — not one specific food or the weight part as much as how all over the place it’s gotten. Some days I barely remember to eat, and then other nights I’m tearing through food like my body is trying to catch up all at once. So yeah, I guess I do feel like my control around it gets pretty shaky at night.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to control the amount of food you're eating during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think that’s the part that feels weirdest to me — not that I’m carefully deciding to eat a certain amount, but that once it’s late and I start, the amount kind of gets away from me.\n\nLike, some days I’ll barely eat or I’ll forget meals completely, and then after midnight it’s like everything catches up with me at once. I’ll be ordering takeout or grabbing snacks, and it turns into more than I meant it to be. I’m not sitting there counting it or anything, but afterward I’m like, “Why did I eat all of that?” It just feels all over the place, like I don’t have a normal rhythm with food right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you criticize yourself after these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. Probably more than I should.\n\nAfter it happens, it’s not even just, like, “Oh, I ate too much, that was unhealthy.” It turns into this whole spiral where I start criticizing everything about myself. I’ll be sitting there feeling gross and wired and ashamed, and then my brain jumps straight to work — especially those Providence accounts. I keep replaying every mistake I made with them, like every missed detail, every follow-up I handled wrong, every little thing my manager probably noticed.\n\nAnd then it gets really harsh in my head. I start thinking I’ve blown my whole future over this, like I’m useless, like everybody at work can see I’m falling apart or that I’m not as capable as they thought I was. It’s embarrassing to say out loud because part of me knows that sounds extreme, but in the moment it feels really true. Like the eating thing becomes proof that I can’t control anything, and then the work mistakes become proof that I’m just… failing, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your late-night eating episodes are related to a fear of not eating enough during the day or a fear of gaining weight from eating too much at night?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s exactly fear, honestly. I don’t think I’m sitting there during the day thinking, “I can’t eat,” or at night thinking, “I’m going to gain weight.” It feels less organized than that.\n\nThe thing that really scared me into coming in is just how all over the place my eating has gotten. Like some days I’ll forget meals completely — not always on purpose, just the day gets away from me — and then other nights, especially after midnight, I’m tearing through takeout and snacks like I’m making up for the whole day at once.\n\nSo, I guess it’s more about not having any normal rhythm with it. It’s not really a clear fear of gaining weight or a clear fear of not eating enough. It’s just this swing between barely eating and then eating way too much late at night, and afterward I’m sitting there wondering what happened.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating patterns are influenced by a desire to avoid gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not really, no — at least I don’t think that’s the main thing driving it.\n\nIt’s not like I’m spending the day trying not to eat because I’m worried about weight, or then at night I’m thinking about my body or calories or anything like that. Honestly, the thing that scared me into coming in is just how all over the place my eating has been. Some days I’ll forget meals completely, like I’ll realize way later that I haven’t really eaten, and then other nights I’m tearing through takeout and snacks after midnight like I can’t get a normal handle on it.\n\nSo if weight is in my head at all, it’s not the main feeling. The main thing is that the pattern feels really off and out of control — like there’s no normal rhythm to it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your late-night eating episodes are a result of feeling hungry after not eating enough during the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think sometimes that’s part of it, but it doesn’t feel like the whole explanation.\n\nLike, there are definitely days where I’ll look back and realize I barely ate anything normal during the day — not because I had some plan, just because I got busy or distracted or whatever — and then by the time it’s late, especially after midnight, it’s like my body and brain both catch up at once. Then I’m ordering takeout or going through snacks way more than I meant to.\n\nBut honestly, the thing that scared me into coming in is more that my eating has been all over the place. Some days I forget meals completely, and then other nights I’m tearing through food like I’m trying to make up for it, but it doesn’t feel calm or normal. It feels kind of out of rhythm, like I can’t get a steady pattern going.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the reason you eat so much at night is because you're trying to make up for not eating during the day or is it because you're worried about something else?\n\nPatient: I mean… yeah, sometimes it probably is making up for not eating during the day, but I don’t know if I’d say that’s the whole reason.\n\nThe bigger thing, honestly, and I know I’ve kind of said this already, is that my eating has just been all over the place. Some days I’ll go most of the day and realize I forgot meals completely, like I didn’t sit down and eat anything normal. Then other nights, especially after midnight, I’m suddenly tearing through takeout and snacks like I’m trying to catch up all at once.\n\nIt doesn’t really feel like I’m worried about some specific thing in that moment. It feels more like I lose the rhythm of eating normally, and then at night it just kind of spills over. Afterward I’m like, “Why did I eat all that?” but at the time it feels hard to slow down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating patterns are driven by a fear of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I really don’t think it’s about fear of gaining weight. Like, I’m not sitting there thinking, “I can’t eat because I’ll get bigger,” or checking calories or anything like that.\n\nHonestly, what scared me enough to come in is more that my eating has just gotten really inconsistent and kind of out of control. Some days I’ll forget meals completely — like I’ll get to later in the day and realize I didn’t really eat a normal meal — and then other nights, especially after midnight, I’m tearing through takeout and snacks like I can’t slow myself down. It feels less like a weight thing and more like my appetite and routine are just all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat normally if you didn't worry about your weight at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I think even if I wasn’t worried about my weight at all, I don’t know that I’d automatically eat normally right now.\n\nBecause I don’t really feel like weight is what’s driving it. It’s more like my eating schedule is just completely off. Like I’ll go through a whole day and realize I barely ate anything, not because I was trying to avoid food, but because I just didn’t stop and think about it. And then later, especially really late at night, it catches up with me in this intense way.\n\nThat’s the part that scared me into coming in, honestly. Some days I forget meals completely, and then other nights after midnight I’m tearing through takeout and snacks like I can’t get a handle on it. So I think if weight wasn’t even a thought, I’d still be dealing with this weird up-and-down pattern. It feels more like my appetite and routine are all over the place than like a weight thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would avoid eating during the day if you didn't worry about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’d avoid eating during the day because of weight, even if that worry wasn’t there at all. It’s not like I’m deliberately skipping meals thinking, “I shouldn’t eat or I’ll gain weight.” It’s more disorganized than that.\n\nHonestly, the thing that scared me into coming in is my eating has been all over the place. Like some days I forget meals completely — I’ll be busy or just not paying attention, and then suddenly it’s late and I realize I never really ate a proper meal. And then other nights, especially after midnight, it swings the other way and I’m tearing through takeout and snacks like I’m trying to catch up all at once.\n\nSo I guess my answer is no, I don’t think the daytime eating thing is really about gaining weight. It feels more like I’m just not in a normal rhythm with food at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're losing control over your eating during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… during those late-night episodes, it does feel like I’m losing control a bit. Not in a planned way, like I decided I’m going to eat a ton, but more like once it starts, I’m just tearing through whatever I got — takeout, snacks, whatever’s around — and it’s after midnight and I’m thinking, why am I still doing this?\n\nAnd I think part of what makes it feel so out of control is the contrast with the rest of the day. Some days I’ll forget meals completely, like I just never settle into a normal meal, and then later it’s like everything hits at once. That’s honestly what scared me into coming in. My eating has been all over the place, and those nights feel like I can’t slow it down once it starts.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is what's causing you to restrict your eating during the day?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s really fear of gaining weight that’s making me not eat during the day. I mean, I can worry about stuff, but with this it doesn’t feel like I’m sitting there making a choice to restrict because of weight.\n\nIt’s more like my eating has just gotten completely irregular. Some days I’ll go most of the day and then realize I forgot meals, like I just never stopped and ate properly. And then other nights it flips the other way — after midnight I’m tearing through takeout and snacks, and it feels way more chaotic than intentional.\n\nThat’s honestly the thing that scared me into coming in. It’s not so much “I’m avoiding food because I’m afraid of gaining weight,” it’s more like I don’t have a normal rhythm with eating right now, and it keeps swinging between barely eating and then eating a lot really late.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Yeah, I think I probably would still have the same problem even if I wasn’t worried about appearance or weight at all. It doesn’t really feel tied to that, at least not in the way you’re asking.

I mean, I’m still functioning on the surface — I’m still getting to work, I’m still answering messages, still trying to keep things looking normal — but it’s starting to show in ways I can’t really explain away anymore.\n\nAt work, my manager has started pulling me off some accounts because I’m making mistakes I normally wouldn’t make. Like little details I’d usually catch, follow-ups I’d normally remember, stuff that makes me look careless even though I’m not trying to be. And that’s embarrassing, because sales is kind of all about staying on top of people and details, and lately I feel like I’m either running on empty or distracted by how off my routine is.\n\nAnd then HR got involved after I called the employee-assistance line, so now it feels more real, I guess. Like it’s not just me having a weird few weeks and needing to get it together. Other people are noticing that something’s wrong.\n\nAt home it’s not great either. The eating thing throws everything off — like if I don’t eat all day, I’m irritable and scattered, and then if I’m up late eating a bunch of takeout and snacks, I feel awful the next morning and the whole day starts badly. So yeah, work and home both feel like they’re slipping out of my control a little, even if I’m technically still showing up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating or control what you're eating during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… during those late-night ones, it kind of feels that way. Not every single time like I’m completely out of control, but once it starts, it’s hard to just stop and be like, “Okay, that’s enough.”\n\nIt’ll be after midnight, and I’ll realize I barely ate normally during the day, or I forgot meals altogether, and then suddenly I’m ordering takeout or grabbing snacks and just going through way more than I meant to. It’s not like I sit down with a plan. It’s more like I start eating and then I’m kind of on autopilot for a while.\n\nAnd that’s honestly the thing that scared me into coming in — not one specific food or the weight part as much as how all over the place it’s gotten. Some days I barely remember to eat, and then other nights I’m tearing through food like my body is trying to catch up all at once. So yeah, I guess I do feel like my control around it gets pretty shaky at night.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to control the amount of food you're eating during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think that’s the part that feels weirdest to me — not that I’m carefully deciding to eat a certain amount, but that once it’s late and I start, the amount kind of gets away from me.\n\nLike, some days I’ll barely eat or I’ll forget meals completely, and then after midnight it’s like everything catches up with me at once. I’ll be ordering takeout or grabbing snacks, and it turns into more than I meant it to be. I’m not sitting there counting it or anything, but afterward I’m like, “Why did I eat all of that?” It just feels all over the place, like I don’t have a normal rhythm with food right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you criticize yourself after these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. Probably more than I should.\n\nAfter it happens, it’s not even just, like, “Oh, I ate too much, that was unhealthy.” It turns into this whole spiral where I start criticizing everything about myself. I’ll be sitting there feeling gross and wired and ashamed, and then my brain jumps straight to work — especially those Providence accounts. I keep replaying every mistake I made with them, like every missed detail, every follow-up I handled wrong, every little thing my manager probably noticed.\n\nAnd then it gets really harsh in my head. I start thinking I’ve blown my whole future over this, like I’m useless, like everybody at work can see I’m falling apart or that I’m not as capable as they thought I was. It’s embarrassing to say out loud because part of me knows that sounds extreme, but in the moment it feels really true. Like the eating thing becomes proof that I can’t control anything, and then the work mistakes become proof that I’m just… failing, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your late-night eating episodes are related to a fear of not eating enough during the day or a fear of gaining weight from eating too much at night?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s exactly fear, honestly. I don’t think I’m sitting there during the day thinking, “I can’t eat,” or at night thinking, “I’m going to gain weight.” It feels less organized than that.\n\nThe thing that really scared me into coming in is just how all over the place my eating has gotten. Like some days I’ll forget meals completely — not always on purpose, just the day gets away from me — and then other nights, especially after midnight, I’m tearing through takeout and snacks like I’m making up for the whole day at once.\n\nSo, I guess it’s more about not having any normal rhythm with it. It’s not really a clear fear of gaining weight or a clear fear of not eating enough. It’s just this swing between barely eating and then eating way too much late at night, and afterward I’m sitting there wondering what happened.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating patterns are influenced by a desire to avoid gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not really, no — at least I don’t think that’s the main thing driving it.\n\nIt’s not like I’m spending the day trying not to eat because I’m worried about weight, or then at night I’m thinking about my body or calories or anything like that. Honestly, the thing that scared me into coming in is just how all over the place my eating has been. Some days I’ll forget meals completely, like I’ll realize way later that I haven’t really eaten, and then other nights I’m tearing through takeout and snacks after midnight like I can’t get a normal handle on it.\n\nSo if weight is in my head at all, it’s not the main feeling. The main thing is that the pattern feels really off and out of control — like there’s no normal rhythm to it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your late-night eating episodes are a result of feeling hungry after not eating enough during the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think sometimes that’s part of it, but it doesn’t feel like the whole explanation.\n\nLike, there are definitely days where I’ll look back and realize I barely ate anything normal during the day — not because I had some plan, just because I got busy or distracted or whatever — and then by the time it’s late, especially after midnight, it’s like my body and brain both catch up at once. Then I’m ordering takeout or going through snacks way more than I meant to.\n\nBut honestly, the thing that scared me into coming in is more that my eating has been all over the place. Some days I forget meals completely, and then other nights I’m tearing through food like I’m trying to make up for it, but it doesn’t feel calm or normal. It feels kind of out of rhythm, like I can’t get a steady pattern going.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the reason you eat so much at night is because you're trying to make up for not eating during the day or is it because you're worried about something else?\n\nPatient: I mean… yeah, sometimes it probably is making up for not eating during the day, but I don’t know if I’d say that’s the whole reason.\n\nThe bigger thing, honestly, and I know I’ve kind of said this already, is that my eating has just been all over the place. Some days I’ll go most of the day and realize I forgot meals completely, like I didn’t sit down and eat anything normal. Then other nights, especially after midnight, I’m suddenly tearing through takeout and snacks like I’m trying to catch up all at once.\n\nIt doesn’t really feel like I’m worried about some specific thing in that moment. It feels more like I lose the rhythm of eating normally, and then at night it just kind of spills over. Afterward I’m like, “Why did I eat all that?” but at the time it feels hard to slow down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating patterns are driven by a fear of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I really don’t think it’s about fear of gaining weight. Like, I’m not sitting there thinking, “I can’t eat because I’ll get bigger,” or checking calories or anything like that.\n\nHonestly, what scared me enough to come in is more that my eating has just gotten really inconsistent and kind of out of control. Some days I’ll forget meals completely — like I’ll get to later in the day and realize I didn’t really eat a normal meal — and then other nights, especially after midnight, I’m tearing through takeout and snacks like I can’t slow myself down. It feels less like a weight thing and more like my appetite and routine are just all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat normally if you didn't worry about your weight at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I think even if I wasn’t worried about my weight at all, I don’t know that I’d automatically eat normally right now.\n\nBecause I don’t really feel like weight is what’s driving it. It’s more like my eating schedule is just completely off. Like I’ll go through a whole day and realize I barely ate anything, not because I was trying to avoid food, but because I just didn’t stop and think about it. And then later, especially really late at night, it catches up with me in this intense way.\n\nThat’s the part that scared me into coming in, honestly. Some days I forget meals completely, and then other nights after midnight I’m tearing through takeout and snacks like I can’t get a handle on it. So I think if weight wasn’t even a thought, I’d still be dealing with this weird up-and-down pattern. It feels more like my appetite and routine are all over the place than like a weight thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would avoid eating during the day if you didn't worry about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’d avoid eating during the day because of weight, even if that worry wasn’t there at all. It’s not like I’m deliberately skipping meals thinking, “I shouldn’t eat or I’ll gain weight.” It’s more disorganized than that.\n\nHonestly, the thing that scared me into coming in is my eating has been all over the place. Like some days I forget meals completely — I’ll be busy or just not paying attention, and then suddenly it’s late and I realize I never really ate a proper meal. And then other nights, especially after midnight, it swings the other way and I’m tearing through takeout and snacks like I’m trying to catch up all at once.\n\nSo I guess my answer is no, I don’t think the daytime eating thing is really about gaining weight. It feels more like I’m just not in a normal rhythm with food at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're losing control over your eating during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… during those late-night episodes, it does feel like I’m losing control a bit. Some days I’ll forget meals completely, like I just never settle into a normal meal, and then later it’s like everything hits at once. That’s honestly what scared me into coming in. My eating has been all over the place, and those nights feel like I can’t slow it down once it starts.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is what's causing you to restrict your eating during the day?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s really fear of gaining weight that’s making me not eat during the day. I mean, I can worry about stuff, but with this it doesn’t feel like I’m sitting there making a choice to restrict because of weight.\n\nIt’s more like my eating has just gotten completely irregular. Some days I’ll go most of the day and then realize I forgot meals, like I just never stopped and ate properly. And then other nights it flips the other way — after midnight I’m tearing through takeout and snacks, and it feels way more chaotic than intentional.\n\nThat’s honestly the thing that scared me into coming in. It’s not so much “I’m avoiding food because I’m afraid of gaining weight,” it’s more like I don’t have a normal rhythm with eating right now, and it keeps swinging between barely eating and then eating a lot really late.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still have these irregular eating patterns if you weren't worried about your appearance or weight at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think I probably would still have the same problem even if I wasn’t worried about appearance or weight at all. Then other nights, especially after midnight, I’ll suddenly be tearing through takeout and snacks, almost like I’m making up for the whole day at once.\n\nHonestly, that’s the part that scared me into coming in. It’s not so much about how I look — it’s that my eating has been all over the place and I don’t feel like I have a steady rhythm with it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.     "psychosocial_stressor": null,
```json
    "additional_requirements": [
      "Onset of symptoms not specifically tied to fear of gaining weight or body image concerns",
      "Symptoms not better explained by another mental disorder"
    ]
  }
}
```
